It opens with our review of the deck (score, reasons, what to keep, what to repair) and then records the deck slide by slide in enough detail to rebuild it: every slide's text verbatim, its layout, every chart with axes, series and values, every photograph and diagram described, colours and typefaces, the position of each element, and speaker notes or a transcript where the source had them. ## Review

**7 out of 10, Effective.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear, consistent system: a contents slide (2), tidy section labels, shaded Q4 columns in the tables (4, 5, 7, 8) and red-versus-grey highlighting in the competitor charts (10). The route is logical, from highlights to financial summary, operations, capacity, technology, outlook, key metrics and statements, and the vehicle capacity table with its status column (9) is a standout. Weaknesses are real: slide 3 is a wall of small grey-on-grey text with the highlights spread across wide gaps, the tables on 4 to 8 and 30 to 35 are very dense with tiny footnotes, and slides 9 and 10 pair long paragraphs with small charts. The photo pages (14 to 25) carry only a small caption and no link to the argument, and the charts (27, 28) have no takeaway titles, so the audience has to find the message themselves.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the consistent section labels, the shaded Q4 column, the status table on slide 9, the red-against-grey peer comparison on slide 10 and the smoothed TTM charts on 28.

**Most useful repair:** Rewrite slide 3 so the key figures stand out at larger size with stronger contrast, and give the charts on 26 to 28 headline titles that state what they show.

**What would change the score:** I would shorten the paragraphs on 9 to 12 and enlarge the charts, and add a one-line point to each photo page or cut some of them.

_Confidence medium. 37 of 37 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a letter-style highlights slide summarising 2021 deliveries, cash flow and profitability, followed by a table of contents. It then moves through a financial summary for Q4 and full-year 2021, an operational summary of production, deliveries and infrastructure, and a vehicle capacity overview by factory. Core technology and other highlights cover Autopilot and FSD, energy storage and solar, and services. The outlook section sets out growth and cash expectations. Photos of factories follow, then key metrics charts and the full financial statements, including reconciliations of GAAP to non-GAAP measures. The closing slides contain webcast details, defined terms and forward-looking statement disclaimers. Worth studying are the highlights and financial summary tables, the vehicle capacity table with its status column, and the trailing twelve month charts that smooth quarterly swings.

## Questions about this deck

**What operating margin does the Tesla deck report for Q4 2021?**

Slide 3 states a 14.7% operating margin in Q4, and slide 4 lists the Q4-2021 operating margin as 14.7%.

**How many slides are in the Tesla presentation deck?**

The Tesla deck has 37 slides. Slide 2, the table of contents, lists Additional Information starting on slide 36.

**What does the Tesla deck say about installed vehicle capacity by factory?**

Slide 9 lists installed annual capacity of 100,000 for Model S / Model X and 500,000 for Model 3 / Model Y at California. It also lists >450,000 for Model 3 / Model Y at Shanghai.

**What growth outlook for vehicle deliveries does the Tesla deck give?**

Slide 12 says that over a multi-year horizon, Tesla expects to achieve 50% average annual growth in vehicle deliveries. It adds that the rate of growth will depend on equipment capacity, operational efficiency and the capacity and stability of the supply chain.

### Slide 3

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The highlights page opens with a single narrative paragraph and then groups bullet metrics under Cash, Profitability and Operations headings, which makes the key results easy to scan.
- Review: Weaknesses are real: slide 3 is a wall of small grey-on-grey text with the highlights spread across wide gaps, the tables on 4 to 8 and 30 to 35 are very dense with tiny footnotes, and slides 9 and 10 pair long paragraphs with small charts.
- Suggested repair: Rewrite slide 3 so the key figures stand out at larger size with stronger contrast, and give the charts on 26 to 28 headline titles that state what they show.

**Text**

HIGHLIGHTS / SUMMARY / Cash / Operating cash flow less capex (free cash flow) of $2.8B in Q4 / In total, $1.5B increase in our cash and cash equivalents in Q4 to $17.6B / Profitability / $2.6B GAAP operating income; 14.7% operating margin in Q4 / $2.3B GAAP net income; $2.9B non-GAAP net income (ex-SBC) in Q4 / 30.6% GAAP Automotive gross margin (29.2% ex-credits) in Q4 / Operations / Record vehicle deliveries of 0.94 million in 2021 / Annualized vehicle production run-rate of over 1.22 million in Q4-2021 / 2021 was a breakthrough year for Tesla. There should no longer be doubt about the viability and profitability of electric vehicles. With our deliveries up 87% in 2021, we achieved the highest quarterly operating margin among all volume OEMs, based on the latest available data(2), demonstrating that EVs can be more profitable than combustion engine vehicles. / Additionally, we generated $5.5B of GAAP net income and $5.0B of free cash flow in 2021 – after spending $6.5B to build out new factories and on other capital expenditures. / After a successful 2021, our focus shifts to the future. We aim to increase our production as quickly as we can, not only through ramping production at new factories in Austin and Berlin, but also by maximizing output from our established factories in Fremont and Shanghai. We believe competitiveness in the EV market will be determined by the ability to add capacity across the supply chain and ramp production. / Full Self-Driving (FSD) software remains one of our primary areas of focus. Over time, our software-related profit should accelerate our overall profitability. More importantly, FSD is a key component to improve automobile safety as well as further accelerating the world’s transition to sustainable energy through higher utilization of our vehicles. / While 2021 was a defining year for our company, we believe we are just at the very early stages of our journey. Thank you for being part of it. / (1) SBC = stock-based compensation. / (2) Calendar Q3-2021 was the last widely reported quarter across OEMs

Two text columns on a grey field with a left column of category labels (Cash, Profitability, Operations) at x 4.4% and their values at x 13.4%, and a right column headed SUMMARY starting at x 55.2% running to about x 95%. **Layout**

Single full-width data table on a white page. Title block 'FINANCIAL SUMMARY' with '(Unaudited)' sits top-left at about 4.6% from the left edge and 9% from the top; the table starts at 13.8% from the top and runs to about 92% with left margin 4.6% and right margin about 4.6%. Columns: row label column, then Q4-2020, Q1-2021, Q2-2021, Q3-2021, Q4-2021 (tinted) and YoY. Footnote sits bottom-left above the page number '4' at bottom-left; the faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white corporate financial slide. Background white #FFFFFF. Title 'FINANCIAL SUMMARY' in dark grey #333333, widely letter-spaced capitals, about 1.7% of slide height, medium-weight geometric sans (Gotham-like, GothamSSm Medium). Subtitle '(Unaudited)' #333333 same family and size. Table body text black #000000, light-weight geometric sans (GothamSSm Book) about 1.8% of slide height, right-aligned numbers, left-aligned labels. Header row fill #7F7F7F with white #FFFFFF text. Data rows alternate light grey #F2F2F2 bands with thin white gaps; blank spacer rows are white. Q4-2021 column cells tinted pale pink, about #F2DCDB. Footnote text light grey #BFBFBF about 1.35% of slide height; page number #BABABA about 1.6%. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners; hairline white gutters between cells.

### Slide 6

**Text**

FINANCIAL SUMMARY
Revenue
Total revenue grew 65% YoY in Q4 to $17.7B. YoY, revenue was impacted by the following items:
+ growth in vehicle deliveries
+ growth in other parts of the business
Profitability
Our operating income improved to $2.6B in Q4 compared to the same period last year, resulting in a 14.7%
operating margin. This profit level was reached while incurring SBC expense attributable to the 2018 CEO award of $245M in Q4, driven by the final two operational milestones becoming probable. YoY, operating income was primarily impacted by the following items:
+ further per vehicle cost (COGS) reduction
+ growth in vehicle deliveries
+ improved profitability of automotive leasing and Service & Other business
- increase in SG&A driven mainly by $340M payroll tax on 2012 CEO award option exercise
- rising raw material, commodity, logistics and expedite costs
- increased warranty and recall cost related to a specific batch of vehicles
Cash
Quarter-end cash and cash equivalents increased sequentially by $1.5B to $17.6B in Q4, driven mainly by free cash flow of $2.8B, partially offset by net debt and finance lease repayments of $1.5B. Our total debt excluding vehicle and energy product financing has fallen to just $1.4B at the end of 2021.
6
TESLA

**Layout**

Single white slide with a two-column text arrangement: section labels (Revenue, Profitability, Cash) in a left column starting at 4.6% of slide width, and body text in a right column starting at 18.3% of slide width. The page number 6 sits bottom-left at about 95% from the top, and a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

None apart from a faint TESLA wordmark at bottom-right, set in widely spaced light grey capitals T E S L A in a thin sans-serif, which is a logo. No charts, photographs, tables or diagrams.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background (#FFFFFF) with no decoration, gradients or shadows. Title is tracked uppercase in GothamSSm-Medium, dark grey #333333. Section labels are GothamSSm-Book in near-black #111111. Body paragraphs are GothamSSm-Book in mid grey #6E6E6E with some runs in #7F7F7F. Bullet lines are GothamSSm-Book in pure black #000000. Body text is about 1.8% of slide height and the title about 1.7%, with the title tracked widely. Geometric humanist sans-serif throughout, regular weight for text. No lines or shape strokes; the page number is GothamSSm-Medium in #BABABA.

TESLA

**Layout**

A single full-width data table of 7 columns (row label plus Q4-2020, Q1-2021, Q2-2021, Q3-2021, Q4-2021, YoY) sits below a letter-spaced title block at the top-left, with the title starting at about 4.6% from the left edge and about 9.3% from the top. Footnotes sit at the bottom left above the page number 7 at bottom-left, and a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Table, no chart or photo. Columns: row label, Q4-2020, Q1-2021, Q2-2021, Q3-2021, Q4-2021 (highlighted pink), YoY. Rows: Model S/X production 16,097 / 0 / 2,340 / 8,941 / 13,109 / -19%; Model 3/Y production 163,660 / 180,338 / 204,081 / 228,882 / 292,731 / 79%; Total production 179,757 / 180,338 / 206,421 / 237,823 / 305,840 / 70% (bold); Model S/X deliveries 18,966 / 2,030 / 1,895 / 9,289 / 11,766 / -38%; Model 3/Y deliveries 161,701 / 182,847 / 199,409 / 232,102 / 296,884 / 84%; Total deliveries 180,667 / 184,877 / 201,304 / 241,391 / 308,650 / 71% (bold); of which subject to operating lease accounting 13,636 / 13,602 / 14,492 / 16,658 / 16,160 / 19% (indented); Total end of quarter operating lease vehicle count 72,089 / 83,032 / 95,491 / 108,757 / 120,342 / 67%; Global vehicle inventory (days of supply)(1) 11 / 8 / 9 / 6 / 4 / -64%; Solar deployed (MW) 86 / 92 / 85 / 83 / 85 / -1%; Storage deployed (MWh) 1,584 / 445 / 1,274 / 1,295 / 978 / -38%; Store and service locations 523 / 561 / 598 / 630 / 655 / 25%; Mobile service fleet(2) 894 / 1,013 / 1,091 / 1,190 / 1,281 / 43%; Supercharger stations 2,564 / 2,699 / 2,966 / 3,254 / 3,476 / 36%; Supercharger connectors 23,277 / 24,515 / 26,900 / 29,281 / 31,498 / 35%. Alternating row bands are light grey, with the Q4-2021 column shaded light pink and the header row in mid grey with white text. The highlighted column and the total rows are the only emphasis; no values are estimated.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle text are dark grey #333333. Header row fill is mid grey #7F7F7F with white #FFFFFF labels in a book-weight sans. Body rows alternate between light grey #F2F2F2 and white, with black #000000 values. The Q4-2021 column is tinted light pink (about #F2DCDB). Typeface is Gotham-style geometric sans (GothamSSm), Book for body and Medium for title, page number. Title is letter-spaced caps about 1.7% of slide height; body text about 1.8% of slide height; footnotes about 1.3% of slide height in light grey. Shapes are flat rectangles with no borders, radii or shadows.

### Slide 8

**Text**

OPERATIONAL SUMMARY
(Unaudited)
2017 2018 2019 2020 2021 YoY
Model S/X production 98,339 101,553 62,931 54,805 24,390 -55%
Model 3/Y production 2,686 152,977 302,301 454,932 906,032 99%
Total production 101,025 254,530 365,232 509,737 930,422 83%
Model S/X deliveries 101,417 99,451 66,771 57,085 24,980 -56%
Model 3/Y deliveries 1,764 146,055 300,885 442,562 911,242 106%
Total deliveries 103,181 245,506 367,656 499,647 936,222 87%
of which subject to operating lease accounting 23,250 11,034 25,439 34,470 60,912 77%
Total end of year operating lease vehicle count 66,752 37,134 49,901 72,089 120,342 67%
Global vehicle inventory (days of supply) 28 22 13 15 6 -60%
Solar deployed (MW) 523 326 173 205 345 68%
Storage deployed (MWh) 358 1,041 1,651 3,022 3,992 32%
Store and service locations 330 378 433 523 655 25%
Mobile service fleet 218 411 758 894 1,281 43%
Supercharger stations 1,075 1,421 1,821 2,564 3,476 36%
Supercharger connectors 9,069 12,002 16,104 23,277 31,498 35%
(1) Before we adopted the new revenue accounting standard ASC 606 on January 1, 2018, operating leases included vehicle sales to customers or leasing partners with a resale value guarantee.
(2) Days of supply is calculated by dividing new car ending inventory by the relevant year’s deliveries and using 300 trading days (aligned with Automotive News definition).
(3) In 2021, we began including mobile service vehicles dedicated to tire repair in our mobile service fleet total. Prior period totals have been adjusted to reflect this change.
8

**Layout**

One full-width operational data table on a plain white page. The title block sits top-left at a 4.6% left margin (top of caps about 9.3% of slide height), with the '(Unaudited)' line directly beneath it. The table spans x 4.6% to 95.5% and y 13.7% to 78.3%, with a grey header row, left-aligned row labels and right-aligned numeric columns; blank rows divide the table into six groups. Footnotes sit bottom-left from y 91.9% to 96.9%, the page number '8' sits at the bottom-left margin, and a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

No charts, photographs, diagrams or logos other than the small faint Tesla wordmark at bottom right. The single visual is a data table with columns: row label, 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, 2021, YoY. Rows with values (2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / 2021 / YoY): Model S/X production 98,339 / 101,553 / 62,931 / 54,805 / 24,390 / -55%; Model 3/Y production 2,686 / 152,977 / 302,301 / 454,932 / 906,032 / 99%; Total production (bold) 101,025 / 254,530 / 365,232 / 509,737 / 930,422 / 83%; blank row; Model S/X deliveries 101,417 / 99,451 / 66,771 / 57,085 / 24,980 / -56%; Model 3/Y deliveries 1,764 / 146,055 / 300,885 / 442,562 / 911,242 / 106%; Total deliveries (bold) 103,181 / 245,506 / 367,656 / 499,647 / 936,222 / 87%; of which subject to operating lease accounting (indented, superscript 1) 23,250 / 11,034 / 25,439 / 34,470 / 60,912 / 77%; blank row; Total end of year operating lease vehicle count (superscript 1) 66,752 / 37,134 / 49,901 / 72,089 / 120,342 / 67%; Global vehicle inventory (days of supply) (superscript 2) 28 / 22 / 13 / 15 / 6 / -60%; blank row; Solar deployed (MW) 523 / 326 / 173 / 205 / 345 / 68%; Storage deployed (MWh) 358 / 1,041 / 1,651 / 3,022 / 3,992 / 32%; blank row; Store and service locations 330 / 378 / 433 / 523 / 655 / 25%; Mobile service fleet (superscript 3) 218 / 411 / 758 / 894 / 1,281 / 43%; blank row; Supercharger stations 1,075 / 1,421 / 1,821 / 2,564 / 3,476 / 36%; Supercharger connectors 9,069 / 12,002 / 16,104 / 23,277 / 31,498 / 35%. No chart axes, legend or highlighted data points beyond the pink 2021 column.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white page with no decoration, gradient, border or shadow. Title and subtitle in #333333; table body text in #000000; table header bar #7F7F7F with white year labels; row stripes #F2F2F2; 2021 column highlight in pale pink #F2DCDB; footnotes in #BFBFBF; page number in #BABABA. Typeface is Gotham SSm (a geometric sans-serif): title in Medium weight with widely letter-spaced capitals; body and table in Book weight; title and body text about 1.74% and 1.79% of slide height respectively; footnotes about 1.35%; footnote markers about 0.90%. All shapes are flat rectangles with square corners and no outlines.

### Slide 9

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The vehicle capacity table pairs each factory with installed capacity and a current status column, separating what is running from what is still in equipment test or development.
- Review: Weaknesses are real: slide 3 is a wall of small grey-on-grey text with the highlights spread across wide gaps, the tables on 4 to 8 and 30 to 35 are very dense with tiny footnotes, and slides 9 and 10 pair long paragraphs with small charts.
- Worth keeping: Keep the consistent section labels, the shaded Q4 column, the status table on slide 9, the red-against-grey peer comparison on slide 10 and the smoothed TTM charts on 28.

**Text**

VEHICLE CAPACITY
In Q4, we saw a continuation of global supply chain, transportation, labor and other manufacturing challenges, limiting our ability to run our factories at full capacity.
US: California and Texas
Builds of Model Ys started in late 2021 at Gigafactory Texas. After final certification of Austin-made Model Y, we plan to start deliveries to customers.
Fremont factory achieved record production in 2021. We believe there is potential to extend overall capacity beyond 600,000 per year. We aim to maximize output from our Fremont factory while ramping new factories.
China: Shanghai
Production of both Model 3 and Model Y continued to ramp further throughout 2021. Local production is essential for reducing the cost per vehicle and improving the stability of the global supply chain. Gigafactory Shanghai continues to be our main export hub.
Europe: Berlin-Brandenburg
Equipment testing through the vehicle production process started late 2021. We are still in the process of finalizing the manufacturing permit from local authorities, which will allow us to start delivering German-made vehicles in Europe. These first vehicles will be built using 2170 cells.
Installed Annual Capacity / Current / Status
California / Model S / Model X / 100,000 / Production
Model 3 / Model Y / 500,000 / Production
Shanghai / Model 3 / Model Y / >450,000 / Production
Berlin / Model Y / - / Equipment test
Texas / Model Y / - / Equipment test
Cybertruck / - / In development
TBD / Tesla Semi / - / In development
Roadster / - / In development
Future Product / - / In development
Installed capacity ≠ current production rate and there may be limitations discovered as production rates approach capacity. Production rates depend on a variety of factors, including equipment uptime, component supply, downtime related to factory upgrades, regulatory considerations and other factors.

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white page: a left text column about 45% of slide width starting at x 4.6% holding an intro paragraph, three subsections separated by thin horizontal rules, and a right column from x 54.7% to 95.7% holding a table above a line chart. The section title VEHICLE CAPACITY sits top-left at y 9.3%, left-aligned to the same x as the body column. The page number 9 sits bottom-left at x 3.0%, y 95.1%. The source line is bottom-right under the chart, right-aligned to the chart edge; a faint wordmark sits at bottom right above it. Margins are about 4.6% left and 4.5% right.

**Visuals**

Table: titled Installed Annual Capacity, with columns Installed Annual Capacity (location and model), Current, and Status. Rows: California, Model S / Model X, 100,000, Production; blank location, Model 3 / Model Y, 500,000, Production; Shanghai, Model 3 / Model Y, >450,000, Production; Berlin, Model Y, -, Equipment test; Texas, Model Y, -, Equipment test; blank, Cybertruck, -, In development; TBD, Tesla Semi, -, In development; blank, Roadster, -, In development; blank, Future Product, -, In development. Header row fill #7F7F7F with white text. Body rows alternate #F2F2F2 and white in the model column. No highlighted cells.
Line chart: titled Market share of Tesla vehicles by region (TTM). Y axis labelled in percent, range 0.0% to 2.5% in 0.5% steps with light horizontal gridlines. X axis is quarterly from Q1 2016 to Q4 2021, labels rotated about 45 degrees. Three series with no printed data values; estimates: US/Canada (blue) about 0.15% at Q1 2016, about 0.25% at Q4 2017, about 1.0% at Q4 2018, about 1.25% at Q2 2019, about 1.1% at Q1 2020, about 1.6% at Q2 2021, about 2.27% at Q4 2021. Europe (red) about 0.1% at Q1 2016, about 0.2% at Q4 2018, about 0.7% at Q3 2019, about 0.85% at Q2 2020, about 1.0% at Q2 2021, about 1.5% at Q4 2021. China (green) about 0.02% at Q1 2016, about 0.07% at Q2 2018, about 0.2% at Q4 2019, about 0.4% at Q2 2020, about 0.9% at Q2 2021, about 1.5% at Q4 2021. Legend at top of plot area, horizontal, with line swatches: blue US/Canada, red Europe, green China. No highlighted points. Chart is flat, no fills or shadows.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no imagery. Section title in widely letter-spaced uppercase sans-serif, dark grey #333333. Body text is light sans-serif in mid grey #6E6E6E, about 1.8% of slide height. Subsection headings are sans-serif in near-black #111111 at the same size. Table header uses grey #7F7F7F with white text; table body text is black #000000. Footnote and source lines are pale grey #BFBFBF at about 1.3% of slide height. Page number is #BABABA. Chart lines: blue #4A7FC1, red #C0504D, green #9BBB59; axis labels #595959. Horizontal divider rules are thin light grey #D9D9D9, about 0.1% of slide height. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 10

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Paired TTM charts of delivery growth and operating margin put Tesla against peer OEMs on the same axes, letting the comparison carry the argument without extra text.
- Review: Weaknesses are real: slide 3 is a wall of small grey-on-grey text with the highlights spread across wide gaps, the tables on 4 to 8 and 30 to 35 are very dense with tiny footnotes, and slides 9 and 10 pair long paragraphs with small charts.
- Worth keeping: Keep the consistent section labels, the shaded Q4 column, the status table on slide 9, the red-against-grey peer comparison on slide 10 and the smoothed TTM charts on 28.

**Text**

CORE TECHNOLOGY
Autopilot and Full Self-Driving (FSD)
The team continued to iterate on FSD Beta software, releasing seven updates throughout the quarter. We successfully increased the number of FSD Beta vehicles from a couple of thousand in Q3 to nearly 60,000 vehicles in the US today.
IIHS awarded the vision-only version Model 3 and Model Y a score of “superior” in collision-avoidance functionality and Top Safety Pick+, the highest possible rating.
Vehicle Software
In Q4, we released a new user interface, featuring a customizable app launcher, simplified controls menu and support for a dark mode appearance.  Additional features included a live view of the car’s blind spot when turn signals are active, editable navigation waypoints and a host of new games, entertainment and audio features.  Earlier in the quarter, we added a live view of the car’s cameras to the mobile app when Sentry Mode is active.
Battery, Powertrain & Manufacturing
Manufacturing is Tesla’s critical core competency. While EVs were often deemed structurally unprofitable due to expensive batteries, we were convinced that manufacturing innovation, purpose-built vehicles and factories would solve cost concerns. In Q3-2021 (the last widely reported quarter), Tesla achieved the highest operating margin across all volume OEMs. Cost (COGS) per vehicle dropped to ~$36,000 in both Q3 and Q4 2021. We believe our current projects, including large castings, structural battery pack, 4680 cells and many others, should help us continue to minimize our product cost.

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white page: text column on the left spanning roughly 4.6% to 50% of the slide width, and a stacked pair of line charts on the right spanning roughly 54% to 96%. The running header CORE TECHNOLOGY sits top-left at about 9.3% from the top, aligned to the left text margin at 4.6%. Three text sections are separated by thin horizontal rules at about 38% and 65% of slide height. The source line sits bottom-right of the left column area under the second chart, and the page number 10 sits bottom-left at about 95% height, both on the same left margin band.

**Visuals**

Two line charts stacked vertically on the right half. Chart 1 is titled Vehicle delivery growth (TTM), with a y-axis from -50% to 200% in 50% steps (labels 200%, 150%, 100%, 50%, 0%, -50%) and an x-axis of quarters Q4 2018 to Q4 2021 (13 labels, rotated about 45 degrees). Series: Tesla as a thick red line (about 138% at Q4 2018, about 157% at Q1 2019, about 162% at Q2 2019, about 88% at Q3 2019, about 49% at Q4 2019, about 41% at Q1 2020, about 17% at Q2 2020, about 25% at Q3 2020, about 36% at Q4 2020, about 52% at Q1 2021, about 82% at Q2 2021, about 88% at Q3 2021, about 88% at Q4 2021); nine thin grey lines for BMW, Daimler, Ford, GM, Honda, Hyundai, Nissan, Toyota and Volkswagen cluster between about -30% and +20% with no printed values. Legend sits inside the top of the plot in a four-column grid: Tesla, BMW, Daimler, Ford / GM, Honda, Hyundai, Nissan / Toyota, Volkswagen. Chart 2 is titled GAAP Operating margin (TTM), with a y-axis from -6% to 14% in 2% steps (labels 14%, 12%, 10%, 8%, 6%, 4%, 2%, 0%, -2%, -4%, -6%) and the same thirteen quarter labels. Series: Tesla as a thick red line (about -1.8% at Q4 2018, about -1.3% at Q1 2019, about 0.6% at Q2 2019, about 0% at Q3 2019, about -0.2% at Q4 2019, about 3% at Q1 2020, about 4.8% at Q2 2020, about 6.3% at Q3 2020, about 6.4% at Q4 2020, about 6.5% at Q1 2021, about 8% at Q2 2021, about 9.7% at Q3 2021, about 12.1% at Q4 2021); nine thin grey lines for the other OEMs with no printed values, spanning roughly -4% to 12%. Legend sits inside the top of the plot in the same four-column grid. No points are highlighted beyond the red Tesla series. Charts are drawn with no markers, no gridlines beyond faint horizontal rules, and no fill.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white editorial look with a light grey palette and a single saturated red accent for Tesla. The header CORE TECHNOLOGY is in widely letter-spaced wide-tracked capitals in a geometric sans-serif (Gotham-style, Medium weight) in near-black #333333. Section headings are in a geometric sans-serif (Gotham-style, Book weight) in near-black #111111 at about 1.8% of slide height. Body text is the same family in Book weight, mid-grey #6E6E6E, at about 1.8% of slide height with generous line spacing of about 1.4 lines. Chart titles are Medium weight in #7F7F7F at about 1.8%. Axis and legend labels are Book weight in #595959 at about 1.35%. Source line is #BFBFBF and the page number is Medium #BABABA. Dividers are hairline light grey rules of about 1px in #D9D9D9. Chart lines: Tesla red #FF0000-like (approx #E31937 rendering as pure red) at about 4px stroke; competitor lines grey #A6A6A6-like at about 1.5px stroke. No shadows, gradients, or rounded corners.

- text at x 4.6, y 47.1, w 44.1, h 14.7 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #6E6E6E]: In Q4, we released a new user interface, featuring a customizable app launcher, simplified controls menu and support for a dark mode appearance. Additional features included a live view of the car’s blind spot when turn signals are active, editable navigation waypoints and a host of new games, entertainment and audio features. Earlier in the quarter, we added a live view of the car’s cameras to the mobile app when Sentry Mode is active.

- text at x 4.6, y 73.0, w 44.7, h 19.9 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #6E6E6E]: Manufacturing is Tesla’s critical core competency. While EVs were often deemed structurally unprofitable due to expensive batteries, we were convinced that manufacturing innovation, purpose-built vehicles and factories would solve cost concerns. In Q3-2021 (the last widely reported quarter), Tesla achieved the highest operating margin across all volume OEMs. Cost (COGS) per vehicle dropped to ~$36,000 in both Q3 and Q4 2021. We believe our current projects, including large castings, structural battery pack, 4680 cells and many others, should help us continue to minimize our product cost.

### Slide 11

**Text**

OTHER HIGHLIGHTS
Energy Storage
Energy storage deployments increased by 32% YoY in 2021, mainly driven by strong Megapack deployments. As demand remains substantially above capacity, growth has been limited by supply.  We are in the process of building a dedicated Megapack factory to address the growing demand.
Solar Retrofit and Solar Roof
Solar deployments were 345 MW in 2021, increasing by 68% YoY, with cash/loan purchases accounting for nearly all solar deployments. Solar Roof deployments nearly tripled YoY in 2021 and continued to grow sequentially in Q4. We are making further cost improvements, particularly on installation, to increase energy profitability.
Services and Other
In 2021, Services and Other gross margin increased to -2.7%, the best performance in five years, nudging above break-even in Q4-2021. While used car sales remained strong in 2021, profit contribution from other parts of the business, such as service, merchandise and parts sales have improved as well.

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white page: a left text column (about 4.5% to 50% of width) holds the section title and three stacked sections separated by thin grey rules, and a right column (about 54% to 96% of width) holds two stacked bar charts, the upper one titled Total Energy storage deployments in GWh and the lower one Residential solar deployed in MW. The slide title OTHER HIGHLIGHTS sits top-left at about 9.3% of height, left-aligned at x 4.6%, and the slide number 11 sits bottom-left at x 3.0%, y 95.1%; a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Two vertical bar charts. Chart 1 (top): type vertical bar chart; title Total Energy storage deployments in GWh (centred below the axis, grey #7F7F7F); y-axis 0.0 to 4.5 in steps of 0.5, unitless with GWh implied by title; x-axis categories 2018, 2019, 2020, 2021; bars are about 1.05 (2018), about 1.65 (2019), about 3.03 (2020) and about 4.0 (2021) GWh, none of which are printed as data labels; bars are narrow rectangles with a vertical gradient from light coral #D9A0A0 at top to deep red #8E2B27 at the base; light grey horizontal gridlines every 0.5 units; no legend; no highlighted points. Chart 2 (bottom): type vertical bar chart; title Residential solar deployed in MW (centred below axis); y-axis 0 to 400 in steps of 50, unit MW; x-axis 2018, 2019, 2020, 2021; bars are about 250 (2018), about 148 (2019), about 195 (2020) and about 335 (2021) MW, none printed as data labels; bars have a vertical gradient from mid blue #3E74B0 at centre to dark navy-blue #2B5585 at top and base with slightly rounded top edges; light grey horizontal gridlines every 50 units; no legend; no highlighted points. Divider rules are light grey hairlines. The TESLA wordmark at bottom right is a small spaced-letter logo in pale grey #D0D0D0 with letters T E S L A.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background #FFFFFF with no decoration. Title OTHER HIGHLIGHTS is in GothamSSm-Medium, uppercase, widely letter-spaced, colour #333333, about 1.74% of slide height. Section headings are GothamSSm-Book in near-black #111111 at about 1.79% of height. Body text is GothamSSm-Book in grey #6E6E6E at about 1.79% of height with generous line spacing (about 2.0 of em). Chart titles are GothamSSm-Medium in #7F7F7F at about 1.79%; axis labels GothamSSm-Book in #595959 at about 1.35%. Accents: red gradient #D9A0A0 to #8E2B27 for the storage chart and blue gradient #3E74B0 to #2B5585 for the solar chart. Dividers are 1 px light grey #DDDDDD rules. No corner radii on text; bars have square corners except slight rounding on the blue bar tops; no shadows.

### Slide 12

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The outlook slide is organised under four fixed headings (Volume, Cash, Profit, Product), giving a clear template for forward-looking commentary.

**Text**

OUTLOOK
Volume
We plan to grow our manufacturing capacity as quickly as possible. Over a multi-year horizon, we expect to achieve 50% average annual growth in vehicle deliveries. The rate of growth will depend on our equipment capacity, operational efficiency and the capacity and stability of the supply chain. Our own factories have been running below capacity for several quarters as supply chain became the main limiting factor, which is likely to continue through 2022.
Cash
We have sufficient liquidity to fund our product roadmap, long-term capacity expansion plans and other expenses.
Profit
While we continue to execute on innovations to reduce the cost of manufacturing and operations, over time, we expect our hardware-related profits to be accompanied with an acceleration of software-related profits.
Product
The pace of production ramps in Austin and Berlin will be influenced by the successful introduction of many new product and manufacturing technologies in new locations, ongoing supply-chain related challenges and regional permitting. We are making progress on the industrialization of Cybertruck, which is currently planned for Austin production subsequent to Model Y.
12
TESLA

**Layout**

Single white page with a left-aligned label column at about 4.5% from the left edge and a body column starting at about 18.2% of the slide width, extending to about 82%. The small letter-spaced title OUTLOOK sits at the top left at about 9.3% from the top, and four label-and-paragraph rows (Volume, Cash, Profit, Product) run down the page with generous vertical gaps, all top-aligned. The page number 12 sits bottom left at about 95% from the top, and a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom right; the bottom-right area and the lower third of the page are otherwise empty.

**Visuals**

none. The only non-text marks are the faint TESLA wordmark in the bottom-right corner, shown as small widely spaced light grey capital letters T E S L A, which is a logo and is listed in elements.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradient, pattern, rules, boxes or shadows. The title OUTLOOK is set in GothamSSm-Medium, uppercase, widely letter-spaced, colour #333333, about 1.74% of slide height. No accent colours are used.

Behind him a second worker in black cap and black clothing sits near a white-wrapped wiring or padding element. Composition: landscape crop, worker positioned right of centre, car body framing him diagonally from upper centre to lower right. Lighting: bright, even factory lighting with specular highlights on the blue paint. Dominant colours: saturated cobalt blue #1F4FA3, red #C8102E, dark charcoal #2B2F36, grey #8A8F98. The photo is full-bleed within its frame, rectangular, not cropped to a shape, with no overlay or tint.

Overhead, white linear light fixtures run along the ceiling on both sides, and white and grey structural columns frame the aisle. Red gantry frames and red cable runs appear in the middle distance. Framing is a wide, symmetrical one-point perspective, with the foreground car cropped by the bottom edge of the photograph. 24
T E S L A

**Visuals**

One photograph, a wide landscape view of the General Assembly line at Gigafactory Shanghai. Looking upward from floor level, the underbody of a vehicle hangs from red padded overhead carrier arms that run diagonally into the distance on both sides. The top-centre underbody is dark grey metal with a black battery-pack tray and cable harness at upper left. Below it, a second vehicle body-in-white with a black front end and silver cross-member is suspended lower in the centre, and a third black body sits on a red lift frame further down the centre aisle. The scene has a wide-angle, slightly distorted perspective. Long rectangular fluorescent ceiling panels run along both sides, and grey-white steel structure and racking fill the background. Red safety rails, red carrier frames and red-and-white hangers line both sides of the aisle. Small green-text display screens hang on posts, and workers in high-visibility vests stand by the line. Dominant colours are red, white, light grey, dark grey and black, with cool neutral industrial lighting. The photo is not cropped and is shown as a rectangle inside the margins, not full-bleed on the slide, with no overlay or tint.

**Layout**

Three equal-width bar charts side by side in a row across the slide, each with its own y-axis and rotated x-axis quarter labels, on a white field with no visible title placement beyond the top-left heading. The heading KEY METRICS QUARTERLY with (Unaudited) sits top-left at about 4.6% from the left edge and 9.3% from the top, left-aligned. Chart legends sit beneath the second and third charts, and the chart caption Vehicle Deliveries (millions of units) sits beneath the first chart, centred under it. The page number 27 sits bottom-left at about 3% from the left edge and 95% from the top, and a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Three clustered column charts, all with light horizontal gridlines and no printed data labels.
Chart 1: column chart titled Vehicle Deliveries (millions of units), one series, single blue column per quarter. Y-axis 0.0 to 0.4 in steps of 0.1 (millions of units). X-axis quarters 1Q-2019 to 4Q-2021, 12 columns. Estimated values (about, no values printed): 1Q-2019 about 0.063; 2Q-2019 about 0.094; 3Q-2019 about 0.096; 4Q-2019 about 0.110; 1Q-2020 about 0.087; 2Q-2020 about 0.090; 3Q-2020 about 0.138; 4Q-2020 about 0.179; 1Q-2021 about 0.183; 2Q-2021 about 0.200; 3Q-2021 about 0.240; 4Q-2021 about 0.308. Columns rise left to right, with the highest at 4Q-2021. No legend.
Chart 2: clustered column chart, no chart title, legend beneath: blue square Operating Cash Flow ($B), red square Free Cash Flow ($B). Y-axis -5.0 to 5.0 in steps of 1.0 ($B). X-axis quarters 1Q-2019 to 4Q-2021. Blue series Operating Cash Flow, estimated (about): 1Q-2019 about -0.6; 2Q-2019 about 0.8; 3Q-2019 about 0.7; 4Q-2019 about 1.4; 1Q-2020 about -0.4; 2Q-2020 about 0.9; 3Q-2020 about 2.4; 4Q-2020 about 3.0; 1Q-2021 about 1.6; 2Q-2021 about 2.1; 3Q-2021 about 3.1; 4Q-2021 about 4.6. Red series Free Cash Flow, estimated (about): 1Q-2019 about -0.9; 2Q-2019 about 0.6; 3Q-2019 about 0.3; 4Q-2019 about 1.0; 1Q-2020 about -0.9; 2Q-2020 about 0.4; 3Q-2020 about 1.4; 4Q-2020 about 1.9; 1Q-2021 about 0.3; 2Q-2021 about 0.6; 3Q-2021 about 1.3; 4Q-2021 about 2.7. Each quarter has the blue column first, then the red column. No highlighted points.
Chart 3: clustered column chart, no chart title, legend beneath: blue square Net Income ($B), red square Adjusted EBITDA ($B). Y-axis -5.0 to 5.0 in steps of 1.0 ($B). X-axis quarters 1Q-2019 to 4Q-2021. Blue series Net Income, estimated (about): 1Q-2019 about -0.7; 2Q-2019 about -0.4; 3Q-2019 about 0.1; 4Q-2019 about 0.1; 1Q-2020 about 0.0; 2Q-2020 about 0.1; 3Q-2020 about 0.3; 4Q-2020 about 0.2; 1Q-2021 about 0.4; 2Q-2021 about 1.1; 3Q-2021 about 1.6; 4Q-2021 about 2.3. **Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF fill covering the whole slide. Plain white background (#FFFFFF) with no ornament. The heading KEY METRICS is dark charcoal #333333 and QUARTERLY is mid grey #888888, both in a wide-tracked geometric sans (Gotham Small, medium weight) with capitals and very wide letter-spacing, at about 1.7% of slide height. The subtitle (Unaudited) is mid grey #888888 in the same family, medium weight, about 1.7% of slide height. Axis tick labels and quarter labels are grey #595959 in Gotham Book, about 1.8% of slide height, regular weight. Legend and caption text is grey #7F7F7F in Gotham Book, about 1.8% of slide height, with tracking. Blue series colour is a gradient steel blue from about #3B6EA8 at the base to #2E5C8E at the top, with darker tones near the top of the first chart; red series is about #C0444A. Gridlines are thin light grey #D9D9D9 lines, about 0.1% of slide height thick. Columns are narrow rectangles with square tops and no corner radius, no shadows. The page number 27 is light grey #BABABA in Gotham Medium, about 1.6% of slide height. Body text is small and the heading is the largest text on the page.

**Layout**

Three bar charts sit side by side in a three-column arrangement across the slide, with the left vehicle deliveries chart narrower and the two cash flow and earnings charts on the right the same width. The title block sits top-left at the left margin, about 9 percent from the top, with the subtitle (Unaudited) on the line beneath it. Left margin is about 4.6 percent; each chart has its axis-label row at the bottom and its legend or axis title below. Page number 28 sits bottom-left and a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right; there is no footer text or source line.

**Visuals**

Three vertical clustered bar charts with category axes of quarters 1Q-2019 to 4Q-2021 (12 quarters, labels rotated 90 degrees).
Chart 1, left: Vehicle Deliveries (millions of units), single series, blue vertical gradient bars from about #3A6494 at the base to about #4A7CB8 at the top; value axis 0.0 to 1.0 in steps of 0.2 with light grey horizontal gridlines; no printed data labels. Estimated values: 1Q-2019 about 0.28, 2Q-2019 about 0.33, 3Q-2019 about 0.35, 4Q-2019 about 0.36, 1Q-2020 about 0.39, 2Q-2020 about 0.38, 3Q-2020 about 0.43, 4Q-2020 about 0.50, 1Q-2021 about 0.59, 2Q-2021 about 0.70, 3Q-2021 about 0.81, 4Q-2021 about 0.94. No legend; axis title below the chart.
Chart 2, centre: value axis -12.0 to 12.0 in steps of 2.0 with light grey gridlines and a zero line; two series. Series Operating Cash Flow ($B) in blue #3B6FAF; series Free Cash Flow ($B) in red #B03A3A; each quarter has a blue bar then a red bar. No printed values; estimates: OCF 1Q-2019 about 1.8, 2Q-2019 about 2.8, 3Q-2019 about 2.2, 4Q-2019 about 2.3, 1Q-2020 about 2.5, 2Q-2020 about 2.6, 3Q-2020 about 4.3, 4Q-2020 about 5.9, 1Q-2021 about 8.0, 2Q-2021 about 9.1, 3Q-2021 about 9.9, 4Q-2021 about 11.5. FCF 1Q-2019 about 0.1, 2Q-2019 about 1.4, 3Q-2019 about 0.9, 4Q-2019 about 1.0, 1Q-2020 about 1.0, 2Q-2020 about 0.8, 3Q-2020 about 1.9, 4Q-2020 about 2.7, 1Q-2021 about 3.9, 2Q-2021 about 4.1, 3Q-2021 about 4.0, 4Q-2021 about 5.0. Legend below with blue and red square markers.
Chart 3, right: value axis -12.0 to 12.0 in steps of 2.0 with light grey gridlines and a zero line; two series. Series Net Income ($B) in blue #3B6FAF; series Adjusted EBITDA ($B) in red #B03A3A. No printed values; estimates: Net Income 1Q-2019 about -1.0, 2Q-2019 about -0.6, 3Q-2019 about -0.8, 4Q-2019 about -0.9, 1Q-2020 about -0.1, 2Q-2020 about 0.3, 3Q-2020 about 0.5, 4Q-2020 about 0.7, 1Q-2021 about 1.0, 2Q-2021 about 2.1, 3Q-2021 about 3.4, 4Q-2021 about 5.5. Adjusted EBITDA 1Q-2019 about 2.5, 2Q-2019 about 2.9, 3Q-2019 about 2.8, 4Q-2019 about 2.9, 1Q-2020 about 3.7, 2Q-2020 about 4.4, 3Q-2020 about 5.1, 4Q-2020 about 5.7, 1Q-2021 about 6.6, 2Q-2021 about 8.0, 3Q-2021 about 9.3, 4Q-2021 about 11.5. Negative net income bars hang below zero. Legend below with blue and red square markers. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF fill covering the whole slide. Plain white background, flat and minimal, with no gradients on the slide itself beyond the subtle vertical gradient in the left chart bars. Title KEY METRICS in widely letter-spaced GothamSSm-Medium capitals, dark grey #333333, followed by TRAILING 12 MONTHS (TTM) in the same face and tracking in mid grey #888888. Subtitle (Unaudited) in GothamSSm-Medium, #888888. Axis tick labels and category labels in GothamSSm-Book, #595959. Chart legends and axis titles in GothamSSm-Book, #7F7F7F, with wide letter-spacing. Title text is about 1.7 percent of slide height; body and axis text about 1.8 percent. Gridlines are thin light grey #D9D9D9 horizontal lines. Bars are flat rectangles with square corners and no shadows. Accent colours are blue #3B6FAF and red #B03A3A. Page number in GothamSSm-Medium #BABABA. TESLA wordmark in pale grey #D9D9D9 with wide letter spacing.

**Layout**

A single full-width financial table on a white page, left-aligned at about 4.6% margin, with the title block 'BALANCE SHEET (Unaudited)' top-left above the table. Column headers run across the top with five period columns right-aligned, the last column (31-Dec-21) highlighted in pink; section bands for ASSETS and LIABILITIES AND EQUITY span the table. The slide number '31' sits bottom-left and a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Table: columns are the row label column plus five right-aligned period columns headed 31-Dec-20, 31-Mar-21, 30-Jun-21, 30-Sep-21 and 31-Dec-21, with the values listed row by row in the text above. Rows are thin light grey hairline rules. Totals (Total current assets, Total assets, Total current liabilities, Total liabilities, Total liabilities and equity, Total debt excluding vehicle and energy product financing) are bold. The 31-Dec-21 column is shaded pink, and the grey section bands mark ASSETS and LIABILITIES AND EQUITY. No charts or images.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background with no gradient. **Layout**

Single full-width table slide on a white field: a letter-spaced title block sits top-left at about 9.3% from the top with its left edge aligned to the table at 4.6% from the left margin. Below it a five-year table runs the full content width, with row labels left-aligned and the 2017 to 2021 figures right-aligned in columns, and a light grey band behind four blank separator rows. The page number sits bottom-left at about 95% down and a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right, both at the footer baseline; there is no body text beyond the table.

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background with no gradient or imagery. Title in letter-spaced uppercase Gotham-style geometric sans, medium weight, dark grey #333333, about 1.7% of slide height. Table body in Gotham Book, black #000000, about 1.5% of slide height; bold-labelled rows use Gotham Medium. Light grey banding #F2F2F2 behind four blank separator rows. The 2021 column is filled pale pink #F4DADC. Hairline row rules in light grey #D9D9D9, square corners, no shadows. Page number in Gotham Medium #BABABA; wordmark in pale grey #C8C8C8.

**Layout**

Single white slide with a letter-spaced uppercase heading at the top left and four stacked financial tables left-aligned to a common left margin of about 4.6 percent of width, spanning to about 95 percent of width. Four table blocks are stacked vertically with a blank gap between them, and a light grey footer line sits at the bottom with the page number at the far left and the note beside it, plus a faint wordmark at the bottom right.

**Visuals**

Four plain text tables, no chart or graphic. Table 1 (Rows: Net cash (used in) provided by operating activities (GAAP); Capital expenditures; Free cash flow (non-GAAP)) columns 2Q-2018 to 4Q-2021 in millions of USD. Table 2 (Rows: Net (loss) income attributable to common stockholders (GAAP); Interest expense; Provision for income taxes; Depreciation, amortization and impairment; Stock-based compensation expense; Adjusted EBITDA (non-GAAP)) columns 2Q-2018 to 4Q-2021. Table 3 (Rows: Net cash provided by operating activities - TTM (GAAP); Capital expenditures – TTM; Free cash flow - TTM (non-GAAP)) columns 1Q-2019 to 4Q-2021. Table 4 (Rows: Net (loss) income attributable to common stockholders - TTM (GAAP); Interest expense - TTM; Provision for income taxes - TTM; Depreciation, amortization and impairment - TTM; Stock-based compensation expense - TTM; Adjusted EBITDA - TTM (non-GAAP)) columns 1Q-2019 to 4Q-2021. Negative values are in parentheses. Two light grey #F2F2F2 bands sit behind the blank row gaps between tables; no rules otherwise, only faint hairlines under row labels.

**Design**

Background white #FFFFFF. Heading in dark grey #333333, Gotham Book/Medium style geometric sans-serif, uppercase, very wide letter-spacing, about 1.74 percent of slide height. Table text in black #000000, Gotham Book sans-serif, regular weight, about 1.46 percent of slide height. Footer page number and note in light grey #999999. Faint wordmark in light grey #CCCCCC. Thin light grey hairlines #D9D9D9 separate rows. Grey bands #F2F2F2. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 36

**Text**

ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
WEBCAST INFORMATION
Tesla will provide a live webcast of its fourth quarter 2021 financial results conference call beginning at 4:30 p.m. CT on January 26, 2022 at ir.tesla.com. This webcast will also be available for replay for approximately one year thereafter.
CERTAIN TERMS
When used in this update, certain terms have the following meanings. Our vehicle deliveries include only vehicles that have been transferred to end customers with all paperwork correctly completed. Our energy product deployment volume includes both customer units installed and equipment sales; we report installations at time of commissioning for storage projects or inspection for solar projects, and equipment sales at time of delivery. "Adjusted EBITDA" is equal to (i) net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders before (ii)(a) interest expense, (b) provision for income taxes, (c) depreciation, amortization and impairment and (d) stock-based compensation expense, which is the same measurement for this term pursuant to the performance-based stock option award granted to our CEO in 2018. "Free cash flow" is operating cash flow less capital expenditures. Average cost per vehicle is cost of automotive sales divided by new vehicle deliveries (excluding leases). “Days sales outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts receivable, net for the period divided by (ii) total revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days payable outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts payable for the period divided by (ii) total cost of revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days of supply” is calculated by dividing new car ending inventory by the relevant quarter’s deliveries and using 75 trading days.
NON-GAAP FINANCIAL INFORMATION
Consolidated financial information has been presented in accordance with GAAP as well as on a non-GAAP basis to supplement our consolidated financial results. Our non-GAAP financial measures include non-GAAP automotive gross margin, non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders, non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders on a diluted per share basis (calculated using weighted average shares for GAAP diluted net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders), Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EBITDA margin and free cash flow. These non-GAAP financial measures also facilitate management’s internal comparisons to Tesla’s historical performance as well as comparisons to the operating results of other companies. Management believes that it is useful to supplement its GAAP financial statements with this non-GAAP information because management uses such information internally for its operating, budgeting and financial planning purposes. Management also believes that presentation of the non-GAAP financial measures provides useful information to our investors regarding our financial condition and results of operations, so that investors can see through the eyes of Tesla management regarding important financial metrics that Tesla uses to run the business and allowing investors to better understand Tesla’s performance. Non-GAAP information is not prepared under a comprehensive set of accounting rules and therefore, should only be read in conjunction with financial information reported under U.S. GAAP when understanding Tesla’s operating performance. A reconciliation between GAAP and non-GAAP financial information is provided above.
FORWARD-LOOKING STATEMENTS
Certain statements in this update, including statements in the “Outlook” section; statements relating to the future development, ramp, production capacity and output rates, supply chain, demand and market growth, deliveries, deployment, availability and other features and improvements and timing of existing and future Tesla products and technologies such as Model 3, Model Y, Model X, Model S, Cybertruck, our equipment, our Autopilot, Full Self-Driving and other vehicle software, energy storage and solar products and the battery and powertrain technologies we are developing; statements regarding operating margin, operating profits, spending and liquidity; and statements regarding construction, expansion, improvements and/or ramp and related timing at the California Factory, Gigafactory Shanghai, Gigafactory Berlin, Gigafactory Texas and Megapack factory are “forward-looking statements” that are subject to risks and uncertainties. These forward-looking statements are based on management’s current expectations, and as a result of certain risks and uncertainties, actual results may differ materially from those projected. The following important factors, without limitation, could cause actual results to differ materially from those in the forward-looking statements: uncertainties in future macroeconomic and regulatory conditions arising from the current global pandemic; the risk of delays in launching and manufacturing our products and features cost-effectively; our ability to grow our sales, delivery, installation, servicing and charging capabilities and effectively manage this growth; consumers’ demand for electric vehicles generally and our vehicles specifically; the ability of suppliers to deliver components according to schedules, prices, quality and volumes acceptable to us, and our ability to manage such components effectively; any issues with lithium-ion cells or other components manufactured at Gigafactory Nevada; our ability to build and ramp Gigafactory Shanghai, Gigafactory Berlin and Gigafactory Texas in accordance with our plans; our ability to procure supply of battery cells, including through our own manufacturing; risks relating to international expansion; any failures by Tesla products to perform as expected or if product recalls occur; the risk of product liability claims; competition in the automotive and energy product markets; our ability to maintain public credibility and confidence in our long-term business prospects; our ability to manage risks relating to our various product financing programs; the status of government and economic incentives for electric vehicles and energy products; our ability to attract, hire and retain key employees and qualified personnel and ramp our installation teams; our ability to maintain the security of our information and production and product systems; our compliance with various regulations and laws applicable to our operations and products, which may evolve from time to time; risks relating to our indebtedness and financing strategies; and adverse foreign exchange movements. More information on potential factors that could affect our financial results is included from time to time in our Securities and Exchange Commission filings and reports, including the risks identified under the section captioned “Risk Factors” in our quarterly report on Form 10-Q filed with the SEC on October 25, 2021. Tesla disclaims any obligation to update information contained in these forward-looking statements whether as a result of new information, future events, or otherwise.

Our energy product / deployment volume includes both customer units installed and equipment sales; we report installations at time of commissioning for storage projects or inspection for solar projects, and equipment sales at time of delivery. / "Adjusted EBITDA" is equal to (i) net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders before (ii)(a) interest expense, (b) provision for income taxes, (c) depreciation, amortization and impairment and (d) stock-based / compensation expense, which is the same measurement for this term pursuant to the performance-based stock option award granted to our CEO in 2018. "Free cash flow" is operating cash flow less capital expenditures. Average / cost per vehicle is cost of automotive sales divided by new vehicle deliveries (excluding leases). “Days sales outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts receivable, net for the period divided by (ii) total revenues and multiplied / by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days payable outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts payable for the period divided by (ii) total cost of revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days of / supply” is calculated by dividing new car ending inventory by the relevant quarter’s deliveries and using 75 trading days.
